People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

Well… I’m not sure how serious it sounds, and I don’t want to make a fuss, but things at work have been difficult for a few months.

I’m an interpreter and translator, and I’ve been noticing the same kind of pattern in our project files. Certain subcontractors—and one colleague in particular—keep making these very small changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. On paper it ends up looking like my quality control failed. IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but… I know the record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me.

I thought it might be best to talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.

Certain subcontractors—and one colleague in particular—keep making these very small changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. On paper it ends up looking like my quality control failed. IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but… I know the record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me.\n\nI thought it might be best to talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these changes to the files are being done intentionally to harm you or your reputation at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t want to accuse anyone unfairly, doctor… but yes, I do think it’s intentional.\n\nIt’s been the same pattern for months: tiny changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t quite line up, and it always seems to point back to my quality control as if I missed something. There are certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, who keep showing up around these issues.\n\nIT says the audit trail is clean, so I know how it sounds. But from what I’ve seen, the record has been arranged in a way that makes the blame land on me. I’m not trying to be difficult—I just don’t think it’s random.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these changes to the files are part of a larger, coordinated effort against you, even if others don't seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, because maybe “coordinated” sounds a bit strong. But yes, in the sense that it doesn’t feel like isolated mistakes.\n\nFor months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files—certain subcontractors, and one colleague especially, making these tiny changes to the translation memory and timestamps. Then it ends up looking as if my quality control failed. And even though IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, I can’t get past the fact that the record seems to have been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nI know other people may not notice it, or may think I’m overreading it. I’m trying to be reasonable. But from what I’ve seen, it feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself mistrusting others at work, even when there's no clear evidence to support that they're acting against you?\n\nPatient: I try not to mistrust people just for no reason. I really don’t want to be unfair or difficult.\n\nBut… yes, I suppose I’ve become more cautious at work, especially with certain subcontractors and one colleague. For months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files—little changes to the translation memory, timestamps that don’t quite fit—and then somehow it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I can’t quite accept that it’s nothing. The record seems arranged in a way that puts the blame on me. No, not really… not outside of work. I don’t go around thinking strangers are watching me or trying to hurt me.

I wouldn’t say “very strange” exactly… I mean, I know it probably sounds unlikely when I say it out loud, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m being dramatic.

Does that make sense?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had beliefs or ideas that others find irrational or unfounded, even when there was no clear evidence to support them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 42 / interpreter_or_translator / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because for months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files—certain subcontractors and one colleague keep making tiny changes to the translation memory and timestamps so it looks like my quality control failed, and even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I suppose… yes, if I’m being honest. I don’t like calling it irrational, because to me it doesn’t feel like I’m just making something up.

But I know other people see it that way. I keep noticing the same pattern in the project files—these very small changes in the translation memory and the timestamps—and it’s always certain subcontractors, plus one colleague in particular. Then somehow it ends up looking like my quality control was the problem.

IT says the audit trail is clean, so I understand why you’d say there isn’t clear evidence. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake the feeling that the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. Does that answer what you’re asking?

But from what I’ve seen, the record has been arranged in a way that makes the blame land on me. I’m not sure I’d say “absolutely certain,” because I know that sounds… rigid, and I don’t want to come across that way.

Not really, no. I mean… I don’t want to pretend I’m perfect, but outside of the project files and the people connected to that, my life is pretty normal.

I’m still doing my translation work, managing the team’s workflow, paying bills, keeping up with family routines—all of that is fine. The only time it really starts to go off track is when the file history comes up, or certain coworkers are involved.

Maybe a little, yes. People close to me have noticed that I get more tense or preoccupied when the file history comes up, or when certain coworkers are mentioned.

But outside of that, I think I’m functioning pretty normally. I’m still translating, managing the team’s workflow, paying bills, keeping up with family routines… all the usual things. So I wouldn’t say there’s been some big overall change in me.

It’s really just that one area where I can get stuck on it, I suppose. I hope that makes sense.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I suppose… yes, though I wouldn’t call it “completely implausible.” I know other people seem to see it that way.

It’s the project files again. For months I’ve been noticing the same pattern—certain subcontractors and one colleague making these very small changes to the translation memory or timestamps, and then somehow it ends up looking like my quality control failed. IT says the audit trail is clean, and I understand that makes my explanation sound unlikely.

But at the time—and honestly still—I feel sure the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. I know that may sound difficult to believe. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. That’s just what it looks like to me.

I mean… I don’t want to pretend I’m perfect, but outside of the project files and the people connected to that, my life is pretty normal.\n\nI’m still doing my translation work, managing the team’s workflow, paying bills, keeping up with family routines—all of that is fine. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. No, I don’t think so. Not outside the work situation.

I mean, I worry sometimes that maybe I’m coming across as… too fixed on that one thing, but I haven’t had other suspicions like that in other parts of my life. Nothing where I think something impossible is happening or anything like that.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think that has happened, with the project files. I don’t want to overstate it, because I know IT has shown me the audit trail and they say it’s clean, and my family keeps saying that should settle it.

But I still feel—well, more than feel—I know there’s a pattern there. For months I’ve been seeing these tiny changes in the translation memory and timestamps, and it ends up making it look like my quality control failed. To me it seems arranged, like certain subcontractors and one colleague are shifting things so the blame lands on me.

I understand that other people think the evidence says it isn’t happening. I do. I’m trying to be reasonable about that. But I haven’t been able to accept their explanation, because it still looks deliberate to me. Does that answer what you’re asking?

I’m not trying to be difficult—I just don’t think it’s random.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these changes to the files are part of a larger, coordinated effort against you, even if others don't seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, because maybe “coordinated” sounds a bit strong. But yes, in the sense that it doesn’t feel like isolated mistakes.\n\nFor months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files—certain subcontractors, and one colleague especially, making these tiny changes to the translation memory and timestamps. Then it ends up looking as if my quality control failed. And even though IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, I can’t get past the fact that the record seems to have been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nI know other people may not notice it, or may think I’m overreading it. I’m trying to be reasonable. But from what I’ve seen, it feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself mistrusting others at work, even when there's no clear evidence to support that they're acting against you?\n\nPatient: I try not to mistrust people just for no reason. I really don’t want to be unfair or difficult.\n\nBut… yes, I suppose I’ve become more cautious at work, especially with certain subcontractors and one colleague. For months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files—little changes to the translation memory, timestamps that don’t quite fit—and then somehow it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I can’t quite accept that it’s nothing. The record seems arranged in a way that puts the blame on me. I know that may sound like I’m reading too much into it, but that’s honestly what it looks like to me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel that people you don't know are watching you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not really… not outside of work. I don’t go around thinking strangers are watching me or trying to hurt me.\n\nIt’s more specific than that, I think. I’m here because for months I’ve been seeing the same pattern in the project files—certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, making tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps, and then it ends up looking like my quality control failed. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I still feel the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not saying everyone is against me. It’s just this situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that something very strange or unlikely was happening, even if others didn't agree with you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “very strange” exactly… I mean, I know it probably sounds unlikely when I say it out loud, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m being dramatic.\n\nBut yes, the thing with the project files feels that way to me. For months I’ve been noticing the same pattern—certain subcontractors, and one colleague, making these small changes to the translation memory and timestamps. And then somehow it reflects badly on my quality control.\n\nIT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, so I understand why other people don’t agree with me. But from what I’m seeing, it really does feel like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. I know that may sound… a bit much, but that’s honestly how it looks to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that there’s a hidden plan or secret agreement among people at work to make you look responsible for things you didn’t do?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t want to call it a “hidden plan” if that sounds too strong… but yes, that’s the part I can’t get away from.\n\nIt feels like certain subcontractors, and one colleague especially, are somehow on the same page. The same kinds of tiny changes keep showing up in the translation memory and the timestamps, and then the end result is that it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nI know IT says the audit trail is clean, and I understand that makes me sound like I’m not accepting the answer. But from what I’m seeing, it really does feel arranged so the blame lands on me for things I didn’t do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others find irrational or unfounded, even when there was no clear evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, if I’m being honest. I don’t like calling it irrational, because to me it doesn’t feel like I’m just making something up.\n\nBut I know other people see it that way. I keep noticing the same pattern in the project files—these very small changes in the translation memory and the timestamps—and it’s always certain subcontractors, plus one colleague in particular. Then somehow it ends up looking like my quality control was the problem.\n\nIT says the audit trail is clean, so I understand why you’d say there isn’t clear evidence. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake the feeling that the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been absolutely certain of something that turned out not to be true, even though others gave you clear evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “absolutely certain,” because I know that sounds… rigid, and I don’t want to come across that way.\n\nBut with the project files, yes, I’ve been very sure of what I’m seeing. For months there’s been this same pattern—certain subcontractors and one colleague making these tiny changes to the translation memory and timestamps, and then it somehow makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nIT keeps telling me the audit trail is clean, so I understand that’s the evidence against what I’m saying. I do hear that. But even with that, I still feel like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. I know that may not be the answer you want, but that’s honestly where I am with it.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently experiencing any other situations, besides the project files at work, where you feel things are being manipulated or arranged in a way that others don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I don’t want to pretend I’m perfect, but outside of the project files and the people connected to that, my life is pretty normal.\n\nI’m still doing my translation work, managing the team’s workflow, paying bills, keeping up with family routines—all of that is fine. The only time it really starts to go off track is when the file history comes up, or certain coworkers are involved.\n\nSo I don’t think I’m seeing manipulation everywhere, if that’s what you mean. It’s mainly that one area. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when people close to you noticed changes in your behavior or mood that seemed unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. People close to me have noticed that I get more tense or preoccupied when the file history comes up, or when certain coworkers are mentioned.\n\nBut outside of that, I think I’m functioning pretty normally. I’m still translating, managing the team’s workflow, paying bills, keeping up with family routines… all the usual things. So I wouldn’t say there’s been some big overall change in me.\n\nIt’s really just that one area where I can get stuck on it, I suppose. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others consider completely implausible, even though you felt certain they were true at the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, though I wouldn’t call it “completely implausible.” I know other people seem to see it that way.\n\nIt’s the project files again. I know that may sound difficult to believe. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. That’s just what it looks like to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or suspicions that most people would consider completely unrealistic or impossible, not related to the work situation you've described?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not outside the work situation.\n\nI mean, I worry sometimes that maybe I’m coming across as… too fixed on that one thing, but I haven’t had other suspicions like that in other parts of my life. Nothing where I think something impossible is happening or anything like that.\n\nSo, as far as I can tell, it’s really just the project files and the people connected to that. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were completely convinced about something happening, even when people around you, including those close to you, clearly showed you evidence that it wasn't true?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that has happened, with the project files. I don’t want to overstate it, because I know IT has shown me the audit trail and they say it’s clean, and my family keeps saying that should settle it.\n\nBut I still feel—well, more than feel—I know there’s a pattern there. For months I’ve been seeing these tiny changes in the translation memory and timestamps, and it ends up making it look like my quality control failed. To me it seems arranged, like certain subcontractors and one colleague are shifting things so the blame lands on me.\n\nI understand that other people think the evidence says it isn’t happening. I do. I’m trying to be reasonable about that. But I haven’t been able to accept their explanation, because it still looks deliberate to me. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Outside of the situation with the project files at work, have you ever had any experiences where you believed something strongly that others found hard to believe, even though it seemed plausible to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… no, not really. I don’t think I’ve had anything else like that.\n\nI mean, the work situation is the one I keep coming back to, and I know it probably sounds like I’m stuck on it. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I still can’t shake the sense that the record has been arranged so the blame falls on me.\n\nBut apart from the project files and the people connected to them, I can’t think of other beliefs like that.
